\subsubsection{The oxygen rows in the physics document (2026-09-12, user report)}\label{the-oxygen-rows-in-the-physics-document-2026-09-12-user-report}

The user read page 18 of \texttt{docs/EXHALE\_physics\_and\_algorithms.pdf} and found
that "The molecular rows" wrote the four H2/H2+/H3+/HeH+ balances and nothing
of the oxygen-bearing molecules. Confirmed against the code: with
\texttt{thereis\_oxychem} the cell system of \texttt{System\_HeH\_mol\_metals} carries two more
unknowns, \texttt{n\_OH/n\_O} and \texttt{n\_H2O/n\_O}, whose balances \texttt{oxygen\_carrier\_rows}
writes on O1/O1r, O2/O2r, the three H2O photolysis channels and the OH
photolysis, with the H2 row gaining the exchange with the water cycle, free
atomic oxygen closing the family (\texttt{n\_O\ -\ n\_OH\ -\ n\_H2O\ -\ n\_CO}) and the H
closure gaining \texttt{n\_OH\ +\ 2\ n\_H2O}; the reverses come from detailed balance on
the NIST-JANAF Shomate Gibbs energies; the O4 + O6 pair is one channel with
O(1D) eliminated exactly; CO is not a row of the cell system but a
transported carrier destroyed one-sidedly by He+ + CO (Langevin 1.6e-9,
UMIST RATE22 4068) and by Lyman-Werner photodissociation with the Visser et
al. (2009) shielding, the He+ row taking the He+ it consumes at the frozen
CO of the sweep. A paragraph "The oxygen rows" with the two balance equations
now follows "The molecular rows" in \texttt{docs/physics\_overview/ionization.tex},
the oxygen-option subsection cross-references it, the carrier-transport
subsection got a label, and three references were added (Baulch et al.
2005, Chase 1998, Millar et al. 2024). Rebuilt with \texttt{latexmk}: 58 pages, no
undefined citation or reference, no overfull box.

\subsubsection{ESWENO assessment memo (2026-09-12, user question)}\label{esweno-assessment-memo-2026-09-12-user-question}

The user asked what the energy-stable WENO schemes gain and pointed at the
three papers under \texttt{references/}. New memo
\texttt{docs/esweno\_assessment\_20260912.\{tex,pdf\}} (5 pages), written from the
published versions of Yamaleev \& Carpenter (2009a, JCP 228, 3025; 2009b, JCP
228, 4248) and Fisher et al. (2011, JCP 230, 3727) and from
\texttt{src/modules/states/Reconstruction.f90}: the energy estimate comes from an
added nonlinear dissipation term (2009a Eqs. 9, 35 to 37) whose proof needs
only 0 \textless= w \textless= 1 and sum w = 1; the new weight functions (Eqs. 18, 21, 22)
with epsilon = O(Delta xi\^{}2) hold the design order at smooth extrema and are
separable from the estimate; the proof covers linear hyperbolic systems in
characteristic form on periodic grids, with no proof for the Euler equations
or a component-wise reconstruction (their Sections 5 and 6.2); the 2011
closures keep it on a finite domain with nonuniform flux points near the
boundaries; the cost is 15 to 20 percent (2009b Table 1). EXHALE\textquotesingle s WENO3
already uses the 2009a weights and floor and not the dissipation term, and
three properties of the code (finite-volume state reconstruction on a
stretched grid, component-wise, nonlinear Euler) keep the estimate from
carrying over. No code change; nothing measured on this code attributes an
instability to the reconstruction\textquotesingle s symmetric part.

\subsubsection{The continuous transfer equations in the physics document (2026-09-12, user request)}\label{the-continuous-transfer-equations-in-the-physics-document-2026-09-12-user-request}

The user asked for the flux and rate formulas of Caldiroli et al. (2021)
Eqs. (3) and (4) in \texttt{docs/EXHALE\_physics\_and\_algorithms.tex}, to be added
only after checking them against what the code computes. Checked in
\texttt{photoionization\_field\_at\_cell\_HHe} (\texttt{util\_ion\_eq.f90}): the outside column
times cross sections gives tau\_out, the cell\textquotesingle s own densities give dtau, the
working flux is \texttt{F\_XUV\ *\ cell\_mean\_attenuation(tau\_out,\ dtau)\ *\ opa\_pf(j)},
the rate is \texttt{sum(int\_f\ *\ sigma\ /\ E\ *\ dE)\ *\ 1e-18\ *\ erg2eV} and the
one-particle heating \texttt{sum(int\_f\ *\ (1\ -\ E\_th/E)\ *\ f\_heat\ *\ sigma\ *\ dE)\ *\ 1e-18}, contracted with the densities in \texttt{photoheating\_of\_cell}; the
discrete equations already in the document are these lines term by term.
Added at the head of "Transfer along the radial ray": the continuous form
F\_E(r) = F\_E(a) exp(-tau\_E), tau\_E = sum\_a sigma\_a int\_r\^{}\{r\_top\} n\_a dr\textquotesingle,
Gamma\_a and H(r) as Caldiroli\textquotesingle s Eq. (3), with the one difference stated (the
column starts at zero at the outer edge of the grid, r(N+Ng), not at a).
Rebuilt: 59 pages, no undefined reference, no overfull box.

\subsubsection{FUV bands B3 and B4 integrated from the spectrum file (2026-09-12, user instruction)}\label{fuv-bands-b3-and-b4-integrated-from-the-spectrum-file-2026-09-12-user-instruction}

The user asked why \texttt{Stellar\ LW\ flux} and \texttt{Stellar\ FUV\ B3/B4\ flux} are
separate inputs when a spectrum is given. READ from the code: with
\texttt{Spectrum\ type:\ Load} the Lyman-Werner flux was already integrated from the
file when its key was absent, but B3 (1231-1450 A) and B4 (1451-2304 A)
came only from their keys; the analytic spectrum types cannot state those
bands (a power law is the XUV fit extrapolated, a Planck the photosphere)
and the ionizing photon grid does not carry them, which is why the keys
exist. Instruction: integrate B3 and B4 from the file as well; Ly-alpha
(band B2) stays a key, a reconstructed line flux being the better number.

Done: \texttt{sed\_read.f90}\textquotesingle s \texttt{lyman\_werner\_band\_flux\_from\_sed} became
\texttt{sed\_band\_integrated\_flux(w\_lo,\ w\_hi)} (one trapezoid over the file\textquotesingle s rows
clipped to any band); \texttt{input\_read.f90} calls it with the band edges of
\texttt{oxygen\_rates} (\texttt{fuv\_band\_lo\_A/hi\_A}) for LW on any molecular run and for
B3 and B4 with the oxygen chemistry on, when the key is absent and a file
is loaded; a stated key, zero included, always wins (\texttt{fuv\_b3\_flux\_stated},
\texttt{fuv\_b4\_flux\_stated}, \texttt{fuv\_b3\_from\_spectrum}, \texttt{fuv\_b4\_from\_spectrum} in
\texttt{parameters.f90}); the setup report names the source and a stated zero.
Fixed beside it: the derivation ran AFTER the oxygen-chemistry warnings, so
a loaded-spectrum run without the LW key was told "Stellar LW flux is zero"
while the file supplied it; the block now runs before those warnings.
Manual rows of the two keys updated (\texttt{EXHALE\_user\_manual.tex}, rebuilt).

MEASURED (private build, hot-Uranus oxygen case with
\texttt{inputdata/sed/hot\_uranus\_solar\_koskinen2022.txt} loaded, 3 steps): with the
keys removed the run reads B3 4.696E+02 and B4 4.445E+05 erg cm\^{}-2 s\^{}-1
against a hand trapezoid of the same file 4.696068E+02 and 4.444851E+05 (LW
without its key 3.687E+02 against 3.687009E+02); with the keys stated
(694.8, 658782.2) the stated values stand; with B3 stated as zero the band
is off and the report says so. No regression case combines a loaded
spectrum with the oxygen chemistry, so the new path fires in none of them;
\texttt{make\ check} on the merged tree is recorded below.
Documentation of the rule (user instruction, same day): the physics
document\textquotesingle s "Bands stated by their integrated flux" paragraph
(\texttt{docs/physics\_overview/radiation.tex}) now states the two sources of each
band flux, the band integral (its Eq. for F\_band), why the analytic spectrum
types cannot supply the bands, and why Ly-alpha stays a key; the manual\textquotesingle s
\texttt{Stellar\ LW\ flux} row, the \texttt{Oxygen\ chemistry} row and the Tier 2b section
(\texttt{docs/EXHALE\_user\_manual.tex}) say the same at the keys. Both rebuilt.

\subsubsection{The H2 extent against Koskinen et al. (2022) Model A (2026-09-12, user instruction)}\label{the-h2-extent-against-koskinen-et-al-2022-model-a-2026-09-12-user-instruction}

The user asked how far out Model A keeps its H2, then for a digitization of
their Figure 8 and a full record of the present state in
\texttt{docs/koskinen2022\_model\_a\_comparison.tex}. Their text: "H becomes the
dominant species at around r = 2 R\_p, but the density of H2 remains
significant at all altitudes" (their Figure 16: outflow supplies the H2 the
chemistry destroys). Figure 8 digitized a second time, independently of the
2026-09-05 readings (400 dpi, ticks from the print, RGB isolation, every
0.1 R\_p): \texttt{benchmarks/koskinen2022\_model\_a/model\_a\_fig8\_digitized.txt};
the two readings agree to 0.06 dex (H2) and 0.02 dex (H, He); q0(H2) 0.837
and q0(H) 0.028 recovered against the stated 0.84 and 0.026. f(H2) of
Model A: 0.93 / 0.69 / 0.53 / 0.43 / 0.39 at 1.2 / 1.5 / 2.0 / 3.0 / 4.0
r\_base, f = 0.5 at 2.15 r\_base, never below 1e-2.

New section 5 of the memo ("The H2 extent"), figure
\texttt{docs/figures/k22\_model\_a/h2\_extent.pdf} (\texttt{docs/k22\_h2\_figure.py}), the two
stationary-route states pinned under
\texttt{benchmarks/koskinen2022\_model\_a/carrier\_reload\_states/}. MEASURED: the
matched run \texttt{matched\_hnu\_minus\_I} (spherical domain to 7.24 r\_base, Rate/4 +
Mdot) has Model A\textquotesingle s shape, no front, f(H2) 0.88 / 0.59 / 0.41 / 0.31 / 0.27
at the same radii, i.e. 0.05 to 0.12 below, and n(H2) a uniform 0.23 to
0.27 dex below theirs from 1.2 to 7 r\_base. The carrier reload fixture of
the solver series (\texttt{backup/regression/carrier\_elem\_newton}) is NOT that
configuration: Roche domain to 4.71 r\_base, power-law spectrum at 0.048 au
around a 1.2 M\_sun star, \texttt{Rate/2\ +\ Mdot/2}, He 2\^{}3S on, no ionization
transport, no conduction, default atomic rates (a first reading listed five
differences from a diff against the superseded root \texttt{input.inp}; corrected
against \texttt{matched\_hnu\_minus\_I/input.inp}), pinned from a B5g state; in it f(H2)
is zero above 1.1 r\_base (f = 0.5 at 1.072, 1e-2 at 1.097), 12 bounded
passes move that to 1.076 / 1.103, and the fixed-wind fixed point fills the
domain (0.97 / 0.91 / 0.76 / 0.51 / 0.39). So the "walking H2 front" of
sections 8 and 9 is a statement about the fixture, not about EXHALE\textquotesingle s Model
A solution; the next measurement is the fixture re-pinned on the matched
keys from the matched run\textquotesingle s state.

The user caught, from the figure, that the first version of the comparison
was drawn on \texttt{benchmarks/koskinen2022\_model\_a/output}, the superseded
Roche-domain state of the morning of 2026-09-05 (top 4.72 r\_base), and that
the fixture\textquotesingle s domain differs from Model A\textquotesingle s: the section and the figure were
redrawn on \texttt{matched\_hnu\_minus\_I}, both figure scripts now default to that
run, and \texttt{benchmarks/koskinen2022\_model\_a/README.md} (new) says which
directory is which.

Gate of the tree carrying the band-flux change, the H2-extent memo and the

\texttt{build/} rebuilt with the gfortran on PATH): \textbf{REGRESSION PASS (all cases
byte-identical)}, 64 file comparisons PASS with data identical.

\subsubsection{The Model A fixture re-pinned on the matched configuration (2026-09-12, user instruction "proceed")}\label{the-model-a-fixture-re-pinned-on-the-matched-configuration-2026-09-12-user-instruction-proceed}

New fixture \texttt{backup/regression/carrier\_model\_a\_newton} (README there): the
matched run\textquotesingle s \texttt{input.inp} with the stationary keys appended and its final
state as the IC pair. Two changes were needed for it to run:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{input\_read.f90}: the refusal of \texttt{Ionization\ transport:\ True} with
  \texttt{Solver:\ Newton} and no \texttt{Coupled\ carrier\ solve} (2026-09-10) is lifted.
  READ in \texttt{ionization\_equilibrium.f90}: the \texttt{x\_hp\_fixed} block imposes the
  transported proton fraction on every sweep whenever the carriers are
  transported and the state is a restart or carries a background, so in the
  partitioned alternation (P6) the hydrodynamic solve\textquotesingle s sweeps are handed
  the transported proton and the carrier relaxation transports it; the
  refusal\textquotesingle s premise (the last sweep re-solving the proton locally) held for
  the plain Newton finish it was written for and not for the alternation.
  MEASURED on the fixture: over 12 passes x(H+) moves by at most 0.011,
  downward, not to the local root. Manual key row and \texttt{README\_HOWTO.md}
  updated.
\item
  \texttt{src/utils/map\_state\_to\_grid.py} (new; closes the \texttt{interp\_ic.py}
  housekeeping item): the matched state was written on the 2026-09-05 grid
  and the mixed grid moved on 2026-09-10 (\texttt{3414478}) by up to 4.4e-4 in the
  centers, which \texttt{load\_IC} refuses at 1e-10. The tool maps a state onto
  another grid\textquotesingle s centers (rho, p, heat, cool in log against ln r; v, T
  linear; every species as its ratio to the hydrogen nuclei, so He/H is
  exact in every mapped cell; the loader\textquotesingle s He/H check at 1e-6 failed on a
  first version that interpolated the densities themselves, 1.04e-5).
  Round trip onto the old centers: rho, p, T to 2e-4, v to 5e-3.
\end{itemize}

MEASURED (12 bounded passes, \texttt{EXHALE\_JFNK\_MAXIT=40}, 8 threads, 3 to 7 s a
pass, hydro info=0 every pass): the H+ carrier row falls 2.1e-1 -\textgreater{} 7.4e-4

stagnation ending because the hydrodynamic energy row of cell 1 reads
2.073e-4 (tolerance 1e-6) on every pass while the solve\textquotesingle s own residual there

\subsubsection{The base cell\textquotesingle s energy row: diagnosed and fixed (2026-09-12, user instruction)}\label{the-base-cells-energy-row-diagnosed-and-fixed-2026-09-12-user-instruction}

Instrumented on a private copy of the tree: the JFNK\textquotesingle s final residual
evaluation and the outer loop\textquotesingle s certification assembly were captured on ONE
state and subtracted cell by cell. At cell 1 every input (particle count,
temperature, heating, cooling) was identical and only the energy residual
differed (3.5e-7); the conduction source of cell 1 was 5.406e-5 in the JFNK
against 5.371e-5 in the outer loop; and the one input that differed was the
particle count of ghost cell -1 (JFNK 0.545985, outer 0.546468, 8.8e-4
relative), traced to the ghost DENSITY the JFNK\textquotesingle s count was formed from:
1.22394867 (the ghost of the state that entered the sweep pass) against
1.22503037 (the ghost after the pass\textquotesingle s own \texttt{Apply\_BC}). \texttt{newton\_residual}
computed \texttt{rho} and the particle count at the top of each sweep pass and
refreshed the ghosts at its end (\texttt{base\_boundary\_states} reads
\texttt{n\_part\_cell1} of the composition the sweep just left), then handed
\texttt{assemble\_residual} the refreshed \texttt{u} with the stale count; the base-face
conductive flux, formed from the ghost temperature W(3)/n\_part, was one
composition stale. The certification forms the count from the state it is
handed and was right. The ghost-fill hypothesis (\texttt{Apply\_BC} missing in the
outer loop) was tested first and refuted (ghost state identical to 1e-11).

Fix (\texttt{steady\_newton.f90}, \texttt{newton\_residual}): after the sweep loop the
density and the particle count are re-formed from the refreshed ghosts
before the assembly. MEASURED on the matched fixture: the cell-1 energy row
in the certification 7.8e-9 at pass 1 and 3.5e-9 at pass 3 (was 2.073e-4);
the 60-pass run no longer stops at pass 13 and spends its budget with the
H2 row at 4.1e-4 (cell 500, the top) and the H+ row at 3.7e-4 (cell 135),
hydro info=0 every pass, f(H2) at 1.2 / 1.5 / 2.0 / 3.0 / 4.0 r\_base
0.931 / 0.660 / 0.460 / 0.346 / 0.296 against Model A\textquotesingle s 0.933 / 0.691 /
0.530 / 0.430 / 0.389 (the loaded marching state 0.884 / 0.588 / 0.408 /
0.310 / 0.267), f = 0.5 at 1.851 r\_base (Model A 2.15), x(H+) 0.061 /
0.133 at 2.0 / 3.0 (Model A 0.020 / 0.080). State pinned as
\texttt{matched\_fixture\_states/pass60}, figure and memo section 5.4 updated.
Without conduction or viscosity the count enters no row: \texttt{wasp\_full\_newton}
reload data rows identical to the control; \texttt{krylov\_and\_dogleg} 334/0,
\texttt{steady\_species\_rows} 195/0, \texttt{certification} 84/0,
\texttt{carrier\_returned\_state\_acceptance} 36/0, \texttt{steady\_completion\_flag} 3/0.
\texttt{make\ check} recorded below.
